<!-- definition-1: body_md -->
\cite{K1}\label{lca}
        A {\bf Lie conformal algebra} $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ is a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module $\mathcal{A}$ with a $\lambda$-bracket $[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]:\mathcal{A}\times \mathcal{A}\rightarrow \mathcal{A}[\lambda]$ which is a
        ${\bf k}$-bilinear map  satisfying
        \begin{align}
            &[\partial a_\lambda b]=-\lambda[a_\lambda b],   \quad\quad\quad\quad   [ a_\lambda \partial b]=(\lambda+\partial)[a_\lambda b],\tag{conformal sesquilinearity} \label{cs}\\
            &[a_\lambda b]=-[b_{-\lambda-\partial}a],\tag{skew-symmetry}\\
            &[a_\lambda[b_\mu c]]=[[a_\lambda b]_{\lambda+\mu}c]+[b_\mu[a_\lambda c]], \qquad \forall a,b,c\in \mathcal{A}.  \tag{Jacobi identity}   \label{li}
        \end{align}
        A Lie conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ is called {\bf finite}, if it is finitely generated as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module. Otherwise, we call it {\bf infinite}.
<!-- end definition-1 -->
<!-- definition-2: body_md -->
\cite{K1}
        Let $U$ and $V$ be two ${\bf k}[\partial]$-modules. A {\bf conformal linear map} from $U$ to $V$ is a ${\bf k}$-linear map $f:U\rightarrow V[\lambda]$, denoted by $f_\lambda:U\rightarrow V$,   such that  $f_\lambda(\partial u)=(\lambda+\partial) f_\lambda u$, for all $u\in U$.
        The vector space of all such conformal linear maps, denoted by $\text{Chom}(U,V)$, has a $ {\bf k}[\partial]$-module structure given by
        \begin{equation*}
            (\partial f)_\lambda u=-\lambda f_\lambda u,\qquad \forall f\in \text{Chom}(U,V).
        \end{equation*}
        In particular, if $U=V$, then we denote $\text{Chom}(V,V)$ by  $\text{Cend}(V)$. If $V$ is a finitely generated  $ {\bf k}[\partial]$-module, then  $\text{Cend}(V)$ has a Lie conformal  algebra structure defined by
        $$
        [f_\lambda g]_\mu v=f_\lambda(g_{\mu-\lambda}v)-g_{\mu-\lambda}(f_\lambda v),\qquad \forall f,g\in \text{Cend}(V),v\in V.
        $$
        In this case, we denote $(\text{Cend}(V),[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ by $\text{gc}(V)$, which is called the {\bf general Lie conformal algebra}  on $V$.
        We define the {\bf conformal dual} of a $ {\bf k}[\partial]$-module $U$ as $ U^{*c}=\text{Chom}(U,{\bf k})$, where ${\bf k}$ is viewed as the trivial ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module, that is,
        $U^{*c}=\{f: U\rightarrow {\bf k}\;| \text{ ${\bf k}$-linear and }f_\lambda(\partial b)=\lambda f_\lambda b \;\;\text{for all $b\in U$}\} $.

        Let $U$, $V$ and $W$ be ${\bf k} [\partial]$-modules.   A {\bf conformal bilinear map}   $f_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot): U\times V\rightarrow W[\lambda]$  is a ${\bf k}$-bilinear map satisfying that $f_\lambda(\partial u,v)=-\lambda f_\lambda(u,v)$ and $f_\lambda( u,\partial v)=(\lambda+\partial) f_\lambda(u,v) $ for all $u\in U$ and $v\in V$.
<!-- end definition-2 -->
<!-- definition-3: body_md -->
\cite{K1}
        Let $M$ be a finitely generated ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module and $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ be a Lie conformal algebra. $(M, \rho)$ is called a {\bf  representation} of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ if $\rho: \mathcal{A}\rightarrow \text{gc}(M)$ is a homomorphism of Lie conformal algebras.
<!-- end definition-3 -->
<!-- proposition-1: body_md -->
\cite{L}\label{prop-dual}
        Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ be a Lie conformal algebra and $(M, \rho)$ be a representation of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$, where $M$ is free as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module. Let $\rho^*$ be a $\mathbf{k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism from $\mathcal{A}$ to $\mathrm{Cend}~(M^{*c})$ given by
        \begin{eqnarray*}
            (\rho^\ast(a)_\lambda \varphi)_\mu u=-\varphi_{\mu-\lambda}(\rho(a)_\lambda u),\;\;\;\forall a\in \mathcal{A}, \varphi\in M^{\ast c}, u\in M.
        \end{eqnarray*}
        Then   $(M^{\ast c}, \rho^\ast)$ is a representation of $(\mathcal{A},[ \cdot_\lambda\cdot])$, which is call the {\bf  dual representation}  of $(\mathcal{A},[ \cdot_\lambda\cdot])$.
<!-- end proposition-1 -->
<!-- example-1: body_md -->
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ be a Lie conformal algebra where $\mathcal{A}$ is free as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module.
        Define $\mathrm{ad}:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow \text{gc}(\mathcal{A})$ by $\mathrm{ad}(a)_\lambda b=[a_\lambda b]$ for all $a,b\in \mathcal{A}$. Then $(\mathcal{A},\mathrm{ad})$ is a representation of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$, called the {\bf adjoint representation}. Moreover, $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},\mathrm{ad}^*)$ is also a representation of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$, called the {\bf coadjoint representation}. If there is a Lie conformal algebra structure on $\mathcal{A}^{\ast c}$, then we denote the adjoint representation of $(\mathcal{A}^{\ast c}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ by $(\mathcal{A}^{\ast c}, \mathfrak{ad})$.

        On the other hand, by \cite[Proposition 3.1]{HL1}, $(M,\rho)$ is a representation of a Lie conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ if and only if there is a Lie conformal algebra structure on the direct sum $\mathcal{A}\oplus M$ as ${\bf k}[\partial]$-modules given by
        \begin{equation}\label{eq-rep}
            [(a,x)_\lambda (b,y)]=([a_\lambda b],\rho(a)_\lambda y-\rho(b)_{-\lambda-\partial}x),\qquad \forall a,b\in\mathcal{A},x,y\in M.
        \end{equation}
        We denote this Lie conformal algebra by $\mathcal{A}\ltimes_{\rho} M$, which is called the {\bf semi-direct product of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ and its representation $(M, \rho)$}.
<!-- end example-1 -->
<!-- definition-4: body_md -->
\cite{L}
        A {\bf Lie conformal coalgebra} $(\mathcal{A},\Delta)$ is a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module $\mathcal{A}$  endowed with a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism $\Delta: \mathcal{A}\rightarrow \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$ such that
        \begin{align*}
            \Delta(a)=-\tau\Delta(a),\qquad (\mathrm{id}\otimes \Delta)\Delta(a)-(\tau\otimes \mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id}\otimes \Delta)\Delta(a)=(\Delta\otimes \mathrm{id})\Delta(a),\qquad   \forall a\in \mathcal{A},
        \end{align*}
        where the ${\bf k}[\partial] $-module structure on $\mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$ is defined by $\partial(a\otimes b)=(\partial a)\otimes b+a\otimes (\partial b)$ for all $a$, $b\in \mathcal{A}$.

        A {\bf Lie conformal bialgebra}  is a triple $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\Delta)$ such that $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ is a Lie conformal algebra, $(\mathcal{A},\Delta)$ is a Lie conformal coalgebra, and they satisfy the following condition.
        \begin{align*}
            \Delta([a_\lambda b])&=      a_\lambda\Delta(b)-b_{-\lambda-\partial}\Delta(a)\\
            &= [a_\lambda b_{(1)}]\otimes b_{(2)}+ b_{(1)}\otimes [a_\lambda b_{(2)}]-[b_{-\lambda-\partial^{\otimes^2}} a_{(1)}]\otimes a_{(2)}- a_{(1)}\otimes [b_{-\lambda-\partial^{\otimes^2}} a_{(2)}],~~~ \forall a,b\in \mathcal{A},
        \end{align*}
        where we use the Sweedler notation $\Delta(c)=c_{(1)}\otimes c_{(2)}$ (with summation implied) for all $c\in \mathcal{A}$.
<!-- end definition-4 -->
<!-- definition-5: body_md -->
\cite{BKL}
        Let $M$ be a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module. A {\bf conformal bilinear form} on $M$  is a conformal bilinear map $\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot):M\times M\rightarrow {\bf k}[\lambda]$, where ${\bf k}$ is seen as the trivial ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module. A conformal bilinear form $\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$  on $M$ is called {\bf symmetric } if
        \begin{equation*}
            \omega_\lambda(a,b)=\omega_{-\lambda}(b,a),\qquad  \forall  a,b\in M.
        \end{equation*}
        A conformal bilinear form $\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$ on a Lie conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ is called {\bf invariant} if
        \begin{equation}\label{cbf1}
            \omega_\lambda([a_\mu b],c)=\omega_{\mu}(a,[b_{\lambda-\mu}c]),\qquad \forall a,b,c\in \mathcal{A}.
        \end{equation}
<!-- end definition-5 -->
<!-- proposition-2: body_md -->
\cite[Theorem 3.9]{L}, \cite[Proposition 4.18]{HBG} \label{LCB-equ}
        Let $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ be a  Lie conformal algebra and $(\mathcal{A}, \Delta)$ be a Lie conformal coalgebra, where $\mathcal{A}$ is free as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module. Suppose that there is a
        Lie conformal algebra  $(\mathcal{A}^{\ast c}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot]^\ast)$  in which $[\cdot_\lambda \cdot]^\ast
        $ is obtained from $\Delta$ via
      \begin{equation}\label{eq:dual}\langle [x_\mu y]^\ast, a\rangle_\lambda=\langle  x\otimes y, \Delta(a)\rangle_{\mu, \lambda-\mu},\qquad \forall x, y\in \mathcal{A}^{\ast c}, a\in \mathcal{A}.\end{equation} Then the following conditions are equivalent.
        \begin{enumerate}
            \item \mlabel{it:11}$(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot], \Delta)$ is a Lie conformal
            bialgebra.
            \item \mlabel{it:12} $(\mathcal{A}\oplus \mathcal{A}^{\ast c}, \mathcal{A}, \mathcal{A}^{\ast c})$ is a conformal Manin triple of Lie conformal algebras with the conformal bilinear form given by
            \begin{eqnarray}\label{eq-blf}
                \omega_\lambda ( (a,x), (b,y) )=\langle x,b\rangle_\lambda+\langle y,a\rangle_{-\lambda}, \qquad\forall a, b\in \mathcal{A}, x, y \in \mathcal{A}^{\ast c}.
            \end{eqnarray}
            \item \mlabel{it:13} $(\mathcal{A}, \mathcal{A}^{\ast c}, {\rm ad}^\ast, \mathfrak{ad}^\ast)$ is a matched pair of Lie conformal algebras.
        \end{enumerate}
<!-- end proposition-2 -->
<!-- definition-6: body_md -->
Two Lie conformal bialgebras $(\mathcal{A} ,[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta)$ and $(\mathcal{A} ',[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]',\Delta')$
        are called  {\bf isomorphic} if there exists a Lie conformal algebra isomorphism $\phi: (\mathcal{A} ,[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])\rightarrow (\mathcal{A} ',[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]')$
        satisfying
        \begin{equation*}
            (\phi\otimes\phi)(\Delta(a))=\Delta'(\phi(a)),\qquad  \forall a\in \mathcal{A}.
        \end{equation*}
<!-- end definition-6 -->
<!-- remark-1: body_md -->
By Proposition \ref{LCB-equ}, if a  Lie conformal bialgebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta)$ is free as ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module, then  $(\mathcal{A}\oplus \mathcal{A}^{\ast c}, \mathcal{A}, \mathcal{A}^{\ast c})$ is a conformal Manin triple of Lie conformal algebras. Thus we also denote such a Lie conformal bialgebra   by $(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}^{*c})$.
        Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot], \Delta)$ and $(\mathcal{A} '$, $[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]'$, $\Delta')$ be two Lie conformal bialgebras, where $\mathcal{A}$ and $\mathcal{A}^{'}$ are free as ${\bf k}[\partial]$-modules.   A ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module isomorphism $\phi: (\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot], \Delta) \rightarrow (\mathcal{A} ',[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]',\Delta')$ is an isomorphism of Lie conformal bialgebras if and only if $\phi: (\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])\rightarrow (\mathcal{A} ',[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]')$ and $\phi^\ast: ({\mathcal{A}'}^{\ast c}, {[\cdot_\lambda \cdot]'}^\ast)\rightarrow ({\mathcal{A}}^{\ast c}, {[\cdot_\lambda \cdot]}^\ast)$ are isomorphisms of Lie conformal algebras, where $\langle \phi^\ast(x), a\rangle_\lambda=\langle x, \phi(a)\rangle_\lambda$ for all $x\in {\mathcal{A}'}^{\ast c}$ and $a\in \mathcal{A}$.
<!-- end remark-1 -->
<!-- definition-7: body_md -->
\cite{L}
        Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ be a Lie conformal algebra and $r=\sum_i a_i\otimes b_i\in \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$.   The {\bf conformal classical Yang-Baxter equation (CCYBE) in $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$} is
        the following equation
        \begin{eqnarray*}
            [\![r,r]\!]:&=& \sum_{i,j}([{a_i}_\mu a_j]\otimes b_i\otimes b_j|_{\mu=\mathrm{id}\otimes \partial\otimes \mathrm{id}}\\
            &&-a_i\otimes [{a_j}_\mu b_i]\otimes b_j|_{\mu=\mathrm{id}\otimes \mathrm{id}\otimes \partial}-a_i\otimes a_j\otimes [{b_j}_\mu b_i]|_{\mu=\mathrm{id}\otimes \partial\otimes \mathrm{id}})\\
            &\equiv &0 \;\; (\text{mod} ~~\partial \cdot (\mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A})),
        \end{eqnarray*}
        i.e., $[\![r,r]\!]$ belongs to the image of $\partial$ under the module action of ${\bf k}[\partial]$ on $\mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$ given by $ \partial \cdot (a\otimes b\otimes c)=\partial^{\otimes^3}(a\otimes b\otimes c)$ for all $a$, $b$, $c\in \mathcal{A}$.
<!-- end definition-7 -->
<!-- definition-8: body_md -->
\cite{L}
    Let $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ be a Lie conformal algebra and $r=\sum_i a_i\otimes b_i\in \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$. If $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot], \Delta_r)$ is a Lie conformal bialgebra, where $\Delta_r$ is defined by
    \begin{eqnarray}\mlabel{coboundary}
        \Delta_r(a)=a_{-\partial^{\otimes^2}} r=\sum_i ([a_{-\partial^{\otimes^2}} a_i]\otimes b_i+a_i\otimes [a_{-\partial^{\otimes^2}} b_i]),\qquad\forall a\in \mathcal{A},
    \end{eqnarray}
    then  $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta_r)$ is called a {\bf coboundary Lie conformal bialgebra}.
<!-- end definition-8 -->
<!-- remark-2: body_md -->
\label{rmk-11}
Let $r_1$, $r_2\in \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$. By the CCYBE in
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ and Eq. (\ref{coboundary}), it is
straightforward to show that if $r_1\equiv
r_2~~\text{mod}~~(\partial\otimes \mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes
\partial)(\mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A})$, then $[\![r_1,r_1]\!]\equiv[\![r_2,r_2]\!]\quad
(\mathrm{mod}~\partial\cdot(\mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}))$ and
$\Delta_{r_1}=\Delta_{r_2}$.
<!-- end remark-2 -->
<!-- proposition-3: body_md -->
\cite[Theorem 3.4]{L}\label{prop-cob}
    Let $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ be a Lie conformal algebra and $r\in \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$. Then $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot], \Delta_r)$ is a coboundary Lie conformal bialgebra if and only if
    $r$ satisfies
    $$a_\lambda [\![r,r]\!]|_{\lambda=\partial^{\otimes^3}}=0,\qquad\forall a\in \mathcal{A},$$
                                                                                
    and
    the symmetric part of $r$ is {$\mathcal{A}$-invariant}, where  the action of $\mathcal{A}$  on $\mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$  is given by
    $$
    a_\lambda (b\otimes c\otimes d)=[a_\lambda b]\otimes c\otimes d +b\otimes [a_\lambda c]\otimes d+b\otimes c\otimes [a_\lambda d],
    \qquad \forall a,b,c,d\in A.
    $$
<!-- end proposition-3 -->
<!-- pdef-1: body_md -->
\cite{L} Let $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ be a Lie conformal algebra and $r\in \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$. If $r$ is a solution of the CCYBE in $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ and the
     symmetric part of $r$ is $\mathcal{A}$-invariant, then $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda\cdot], \Delta_r)$ is a Lie conformal bialgebra, which is called a {\bf quasi-triangular Lie conformal bialgebra},
     where $\Delta_r$ is given by Eq. (\ref{coboundary}). In particular, if $r$ is a skew-symmetric solution of the CCYBE in $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$, then $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda\cdot], \Delta_r)$ is called a {\bf triangular Lie conformal bialgebra}.
<!-- end pdef-1 -->
<!-- remark-3: body_md -->
\label{rmk-2}
Let $r_1$, $r_2\in \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$. If $r_1\equiv r_2
~~\text{mod}~~(\partial\otimes \mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes
\partial)(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A})$, then it is straightforward to show that
${r_1}_+={r_2}_+$ and ${r_1}_-={r_2}_-$.

Let $\{e_1, \ldots, e_n\}$ be a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-basis of $\mathcal{A}$
and $\{e_1^\ast, \ldots, e_n^\ast\}$ be the dual ${\bf
k}[\partial]$-basis of $\mathcal{A}^{\ast c}$.                  
Set
$$r=\sum_{i=1}^n\sum_{j=1}^nf_{ij}(\partial\otimes \mathrm{id},\mathrm{id}\otimes
\partial)e_i\otimes e_j.$$ Then for any $k\in \{1, \ldots, n\}$,
we obtain
\begin{eqnarray}
r_+(e_k^\ast)=\sum_{j=1}^nf_{kj}(-\partial, \partial)e_j,\;\;\;r_-(e_k^\ast)=-\sum_{i=1}^nf_{ik}(\partial, -\partial)e_i.
\end{eqnarray}
Therefore, $r$ $\text{mod}~~(\partial\otimes \mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes \partial)(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A})$ is determined by $r_+$ or $r_-$.

By the discussion above, if
$r_+(e_k^\ast)=\sum_{j=1}^ng_{kj}(\partial)e_j$ for $k\in \{1,
\ldots, n\}$ where $g_{kj}(\partial)\in {\bf k}[\partial]$, then
we get $r\equiv \sum_{k=1}^n\sum_{j=1}^ng_{kj}(\mathrm{id}\otimes
\partial)e_k\otimes e_j$ $\text{mod}~~(\partial\otimes
\mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes \partial)(\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{A})$. By Remark \ref{rmk-11}, in
the study of coboundary Lie conformal bialgebras, we choose
$$r=\sum_{k=1}^n\sum_{j=1}^ng_{kj}(\mathrm{id}\otimes \partial)e_k\otimes
e_j=\sum_{k=1}^n e_k\otimes r_+(e_k^*).$$
<!-- end remark-3 -->
<!-- proposition-4: body_md -->
\label{prop-1}
With the assumption above, define a $\lambda$-bracket on $\mathcal{A}^{*c}$ by
\begin{equation}\label{acl}
    [x_\lambda y]^r={\rm ad}^*(r_+(x))_\lambda y-{\rm ad}^*(r_-(y))_{-\lambda-\partial}x,\qquad   \forall x,y\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}.
\end{equation}
Then $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^r)$ is a Lie conformal algebra if and only if $(\mathcal{A}, \Delta_r)$ is a Lie conformal coalgebra, where $\Delta_r$ is given by Eq. (\ref{coboundary}).
<!-- end proposition-4 -->
<!-- proof-1: body_md -->
For all $x$, $y\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}$, $a\in \mathcal{A}$ and $r=\sum_i a_i\otimes b_i\in\mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$, we have
\begin{eqnarray*}
    \langle [x_\lambda y]^r,a \rangle_\mu&=&\langle {\rm ad}^*(r_+(x))_\lambda y-{\rm ad}^*(r_-(y))_{-\lambda-\partial}x,a\rangle_\mu\\
    &=&-\langle y, [{r_+(x)}_\lambda a]\rangle_{\mu-\lambda}+\langle x, [{r_-(y)}_{-\lambda+\mu} a]\rangle_\lambda\\
    &=&\sum_i\langle  x\otimes y,-a_i\otimes [{b_i}_{\lambda}a ]-[{a_i}_{\mu-\lambda}a]\otimes b_i\rangle_{\lambda,\mu-\lambda}\\
    &=&\sum_i\langle  x\otimes y,a_i\otimes [a_{-\mu}b_i]+[a_{-\mu}a_i]\otimes b_i\rangle_{\lambda,\mu-\lambda}\\
    &=&\langle x\otimes y,\Delta_r(a)\rangle_{\lambda,\mu-\lambda}.
\end{eqnarray*}
Then this conclusion follows directly by
\cite[Proposition 2.13]{L}.
<!-- end proof-1 -->
<!-- lemma-1: body_md -->
\label{I}
Let $ (\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ be a Lie conformal algebra, where
$\mathcal{A}$ is free as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module, and $r\in \mathcal{A}\otimes
\mathcal{A}$. Then the following conclusions hold.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item \label{item1}$I=I^*$. In particular, if $I$ is an isomorphism of Lie conformal algebras, then $(I^{-1})^*=(I^*)^{-1}=I^{-1}$.
    \item \label{item2}The symmetric part of $r$ is $\mathcal{A}$-invariant if and only if
   \begin{equation}\label{eq:rr1}
   	 I\circ {\rm ad}^*(a)_\lambda={\rm ad}(a)_\lambda\circ I,\qquad \forall a\in \mathcal{A}.
   	 \end{equation}
 
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end lemma-1 -->
<!-- proof-2: body_md -->
Let $r=\sum_i a_i \otimes b_i$.

(\ref{item1})
   Let $x$, $y\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}$. Then we obtain
    \begin{eqnarray*}
        \langle I^*(x),y\rangle_\lambda&=&\langle x,I(y)\rangle_\lambda=\sum_i\langle x,\langle y,a_i\rangle_{-\partial}b_i+\langle y,b_i\rangle_{-\partial}a_i\rangle_\lambda\\
        &=&\sum_i(\langle x,b_i\rangle_\lambda\langle y,a_i\rangle_{-\lambda}+\langle x,a_i\rangle_\lambda\langle y,b_i\rangle_{-\lambda})\\
        &=&\langle r_+(x)-r_-(x),y\rangle_\lambda=\langle
        I(x),y\rangle_\lambda.
    \end{eqnarray*}
    Hence $I=I^*$. Moreover, if $I$ is an isomorphism of Lie conformal algebras, then we get
    \begin{eqnarray*}
        \langle x,a \rangle_\lambda =\langle x,(I\circ I^{-1}) (a)\rangle_\lambda =\langle I^*(x),I^{-1}(a ) \rangle_\lambda=\langle ((I^{-1})^*\circ I^*)(x),a\rangle_\lambda.
                                                                                                     
    \end{eqnarray*}
 Thus $({I^\ast})^{-1}=(I^{-1})^*$.                            

 (\ref{item2})   Let $x,y\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}$ and $a\in \mathcal{A}$.  By Item (\ref{item1}), we have
    \begin{eqnarray*}
        &&\sum_i   \langle x\otimes y, a_i\otimes [a_{-\partial^{\otimes^2}}b_i]+ [a_{-\partial^{\otimes^2}}a_i]\otimes  b_i  + [a_{-\partial^{\otimes^2}}b_i]\otimes a_i+ b_i \otimes [a_{-\partial^{\otimes^2}}a_i]  \rangle_{ \lambda,\mu}\\
        &&\quad=        \langle y, \sum_i ( \langle {\rm ad}^*(a)_{-\lambda-\mu}x, a_i\rangle_{-\mu}b_i +\langle {\rm ad}^*(a)_{-\lambda-\mu}x, b_i\rangle_{-\mu}a_i )  \rangle_\mu  \\
        &&\quad\quad+\langle x, \sum_i ( \langle {\rm ad}^*(a)_{-\lambda-\mu}y, b_i\rangle_{-\lambda}a_i +\langle {\rm ad}^*(a)_{-\lambda-\mu}y, a_i\rangle_{-\lambda}b_i)  \rangle_\lambda   \\
        &&\quad=\langle y, (r_+-r_-)({\rm ad}^*(a)_{-\lambda-\mu}x )\rangle_\mu
        +\langle x, (r_+-r_-)({\rm ad}^*(a)_{-\mu-\lambda}y )\rangle_\lambda\\
        &&\quad=\langle y, I ({\rm ad}^*(a)_{-\lambda-\mu}x )\rangle_\mu
        +\langle   ({\rm ad}^*(a)_{-\mu-\lambda}y ),I(x)  \rangle_{-\lambda}\\
        &&\quad=\langle y, I ({\rm ad}^*(a)_{-\lambda-\partial}x )\rangle_\mu
        - \langle   y ,({\rm ad}(a)_{-\lambda-\partial})I(x)
        \rangle_{\mu}.
    \end{eqnarray*}
    Hence the symmetric part of $r$ is $\mathcal{A}$-invariant if and only
    if Eq.~(\ref{eq:rr1}) holds.
<!-- end proof-2 -->
<!-- remark-4: body_md -->
\label{rmk-d}
With the assumption in Lemma \ref{I}, we obtain
\begin{eqnarray*}
    \langle r_-^\ast(x),y\rangle_\lambda&=&\langle x, r_-(y)\rangle_\lambda=\langle x, -\sum_i\langle y,b_i\rangle_{-\partial} a_i\rangle_\lambda\\
    &=&-\sum_i\langle y,b_i\rangle_{-\lambda} \langle x, a_i\rangle_\lambda=-\langle r_+(x),y\rangle_\lambda,\;\;\;\forall x, y\in \mathcal{A}^{\ast^c}.
\end{eqnarray*}
Therefore, we get $r_-^\ast=-r_+$. Similarly, we have $r_-=-r_+^\ast$.
<!-- end remark-4 -->
<!-- theorem-1: body_md -->
\label{qtl}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ be a  Lie conformal algebra, where
$\mathcal{A}$ is free as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module, and $r\in \mathcal{A}\otimes
\mathcal{A}$. If the symmetric part of  $r$ is $\mathcal{A}$-invariant, then $r$ is
a solution of the CCYBE in $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ if and only
if $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^r)$ is a Lie conformal algebra
where the $\lambda$-bracket is defined by Eq. \eqref{acl} and
$r_-: (\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^r) \rightarrow
(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$  is a Lie conformal algebra
homomorphism.
<!-- end theorem-1 -->
<!-- proof-3: body_md -->
By Proposition \ref{prop-cob}, $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot], \Delta_r)$ is a coboundary Lie conformal bialgebra if and only if the symmetric part of $r$ is $\mathcal{A}$-invariant and $a_{-\partial^{\otimes^3}}[\![r,r]\!]=0$ for all $a\in \mathcal{A}$. Moreover, $[\![r,r]\!]\equiv 0$ $~~(\text{mod} ~~\partial \cdot (\mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A} \otimes \mathcal{A}))$ implies that $ a_{ -\partial^{\otimes^3}}[\![r,r]\!]=0$.  Thus, if the symmetric part of  $r$ is $\mathcal{A}$-invariant and $r$ is a solution of the CCYBE, then $(\mathcal{A}, \Delta_r)$ is a Lie conformal coalgebra. Therefore, by Proposition \ref{prop-1}, $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^r)$ is a Lie conformal algebra.
                                                                              

Let $r=\sum_i a_i\otimes b_i\in \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$. For all $x,y,z\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}$, we have
\begin{eqnarray*}
    && \langle x,[r_-(y)_\mu r_-(z)]-r_-({\rm ad}^*(r_+(y))_\mu z-{\rm ad}^*(r_-(z))_{-\mu-\partial}y)\rangle_\lambda\\
    &&\quad=\sum_{i,j}(\langle x,\langle y,b_i\rangle_\mu\langle z,b_j\rangle_{-\mu-\partial}[{a_i}_\mu a_j] \rangle_\lambda    -\langle x,a_i\rangle_\lambda\langle z,\langle y,a_j\rangle_\mu[{b_j}_\mu b_i]\rangle_{-\lambda-\mu}\\
    &&\quad\quad-\langle x,a_i\rangle_\lambda\langle y,\langle z,b_j\rangle_{-\lambda-\mu}[{a_j}_{-\lambda-\mu}b_i]\rangle_\mu  ) \\
    &&\quad=\langle x\otimes y\otimes z,[\![r,r]\!] ~~(\text{mod} ~~\partial \cdot (\mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A} \otimes
    \mathcal{A}))\rangle_{\lambda,\mu,-\lambda-\mu}.
\end{eqnarray*}
Hence $r$ is a solution of the CCYBE in $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda
\cdot])$ if and only if $r_-$ is a Lie conformal algebra
homomorphism from $(\mathcal{A}^{*c}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot]^r)$ to $(\mathcal{A},
[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$. Therefore, this
conclusion holds.
<!-- end proof-3 -->
<!-- remark-5: body_md -->
\label{rmk-new}
Under the condition that the symmetric part of $r$ is $\mathcal{A}$-invariant, the ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism $r_-$
is a Lie conformal algebra homomorphism  if and only if $r_+$  is a Lie conformal algebra homomorphism. Then $I=r_+-r_-$ is also a Lie conformal algebra homomorphism.
<!-- end remark-5 -->
<!-- definition-9: body_md -->
A quasi-triangular Lie conformal bialgebra
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta_r)$ is called {\bf factorizable}
if $\mathcal{A}$ is free as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module and the ${\bf
k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism $I: (\mathcal{A}^{*c}, [\cdot_\lambda
\cdot]^r)\rightarrow (\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ is an
isomorphism of Lie conformal algebras.
<!-- end definition-9 -->
<!-- proposition-5: body_md -->
\label{ppfa}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta_r) $ be a factorizable Lie conformal bialgebra. Then $\text{Im}(r_+\oplus r_-)$ is a direct sum of Lie conformal subalgebras, which is isomorphic to the Lie conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^r)$.   In addition, any $a\in\mathcal{A}$ has a unique decomposition $a=a_+-a_-$ where $(a_+,a_-)\in \text{Im}(r_+\oplus r_-)$.
<!-- end proposition-5 -->
<!-- proof-4: body_md -->
By Theorem \ref{qtl} and Remark \ref{rmk-new}, $r_+$ and $r_-$ are two Lie conformal algebra homomorphisms, which means that $\text{Im}(r_+\oplus r_-)$ is a direct sum of Lie conformal subalgebras. Since $I=r_+-r_-$ is a Lie conformal algebra isomorphism, it follows that $\text{Im}(r_+\oplus r_-) $ is isomorphic to $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^r)$ and $\varphi$ is surjective, which completes the proof.
<!-- end proof-4 -->
<!-- definition-10: body_md -->
\cite{HB}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ be a Lie conformal algebra and
$\theta\in {\bf k}$. A ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism
$\mathfrak{B}:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow \mathcal{A}$ is called a {\bf Rota-Baxter
operator of weight $\theta$}  on $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ if it
satisfies
\begin{equation*}           
    [\mathfrak{B}(a)_\lambda \mathfrak{B}(b)]=\mathfrak{B}([\mathfrak{B}(a)_\lambda b]+[a_\lambda \mathfrak{B}(b)]+\theta[a_\lambda b]  ),\qquad \forall a,b\in \mathcal{A}.
\end{equation*}
Moreover, a {\bf   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal
algebra of weight  $\theta$} is a  Lie conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$
with a Rota-Baxter operator $\mathfrak{B}$ of weight $\theta$,
which is denoted by $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B})$.
<!-- end definition-10 -->
<!-- lemma-2: body_md -->
\label{bb8}
Let $\mathfrak{B}$ be a Rota-Baxter operator of weight $\theta$ on
a Lie conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$. Then
$\widehat{\mathfrak{B}}=-\mathfrak{B}-\theta \mathrm{id} $ is also a
Rota-Baxter operator of weight $\theta$ on  $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda
\cdot])$.
<!-- end lemma-2 -->
<!-- proof-5: body_md -->
It is straightforward.
<!-- end proof-5 -->
<!-- proposition-6: body_md -->
\label{prop-2}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ be a
Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra of weight  $\theta$. Then there is
a new Lie conformal algebra
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^\mathfrak{B})$, where
$[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^\mathfrak{B}$ is defined by
\begin{eqnarray*}
    [a_\lambda b]^\mathfrak{B}:=[\mathfrak{B}(a)_\lambda b]+[a_\lambda \mathfrak{B}(b)]+\theta [a_\lambda b],\qquad \forall a,b\in \mathcal{A}.
\end{eqnarray*}
We call $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot ]^\mathfrak{B})$ the {\bf
descendent Lie conformal algebra} of
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ and denote it by
$\mathcal{A}_\mathfrak{B}$. Moreover, $\mathfrak{B}$ is a homomorphism of
Lie conformal algebras from $\mathcal{A}_\mathfrak{B}$ to $(\mathcal{A},
[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ and $\mathfrak{B}$ is still a Rota-Baxter
operator of weight $\theta$ on $\mathcal{A}_\mathfrak{B}$.
<!-- end proposition-6 -->
<!-- proof-6: body_md -->
It is straightforward.
<!-- end proof-6 -->
<!-- definition-11: body_md -->
A {\bf representation} of a    Rota-Baxter Lie
conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],$ $\mathfrak{B})$ of weight  $\theta$ is a triple
$(M,\rho,\mathfrak{T})$, where $(M,\rho)$ is a representation of
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ and $\mathfrak{T}:M\rightarrow M$ is a ${\bf
k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism satisfying
\begin{equation*}             
    \rho(\mathfrak{B} (a))_\lambda (\mathfrak{T} (v))=\mathfrak{T}(\rho (\mathfrak{B}(a))_\lambda v+\rho(a)_\lambda \mathfrak{T} (v)+\theta\rho(a)_\lambda v),\qquad
    \forall a\in \mathcal{A}, v\in M.
\end{equation*}
<!-- end definition-11 -->
<!-- proposition-7: body_md -->
\label{pp-sprb}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot], \mathfrak{B})$ be a
Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra of weight  $\theta$, $M$ be a ${\bf
k}[\partial]$-module, and $\mathfrak{T}:M\rightarrow M$ be a ${\bf
k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism. Define a ${\bf
k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism $\mathfrak{B}\oplus\mathfrak{T}$ on the direct sum
$\mathcal{A}\oplus M$ (as ${\bf k}[\partial]$-modules)
                                                                       
 by
$$(\mathfrak{B}\oplus\mathfrak{T})(a,x)=(\mathfrak{B} (a),\mathfrak{T} (x)),\;\; \forall a\in \mathcal{A}, x\in
M.$$ Then $(\mathcal{A}\oplus M,[\cdot_\lambda \cdot], \mathfrak{B}\oplus \mathfrak{T})$ is a
   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra of weight  $\theta$ with the
$\lambda$-bracket defined by Eq. (\ref{eq-rep})  if and only if
$(M,\rho,\mathfrak{T}) $ is a representation of
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B})$.
<!-- end proposition-7 -->
<!-- proof-7: body_md -->
It is straightforward.
<!-- end proof-7 -->
<!-- lemma-3: body_md -->
\label{m8}
Let  $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ be a
Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra of weight  $\theta$, $(M,\rho) $ be a representation
of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ and $\beta:M\rightarrow M$ be a ${\bf
k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism, where $M$ is free as a ${\bf
k}[\partial]$-module. Then $(M^{*c},\rho^*,\beta^*)$
is a representation of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ if and only
if $\beta$ satisfies the following condition:
\begin{equation}\label{eqtb}
    \beta(\rho(\mathfrak{B}( a))_\lambda v)=\rho(\mathfrak{B} (a))_\lambda \beta( v)+\beta(\rho (a)_\lambda \beta (v)) +\theta\rho(a)_\lambda \beta (v),\qquad \forall a\in \mathcal{A},\;v\in M.
\end{equation}
In particular,  if $\mathcal{A}$ is also free as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module, then for a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism $\mathfrak{T}:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow \mathcal{A}$, $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},ad^*,\mathfrak{T}^*)$ is a representation of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ if and only if $\mathfrak{T}$ satisfies the following condition.
\begin{equation}\label{eqaa}
    \mathfrak{T}([\mathfrak{B}( a)_\lambda b])=[\mathfrak{B} (a)_\lambda \mathfrak{T}( b)]+\mathfrak{T}([a_\lambda \mathfrak{T} (b)] )+\theta[a_\lambda \mathfrak{T} (b)],\qquad \forall a,b\in \mathcal{A}.
\end{equation}
<!-- end lemma-3 -->
<!-- proof-8: body_md -->
For all $a\in \mathcal{A}$, $v\in M$ and $f\in M^{\ast c}$, we have
\begin{align*}
    & &\langle  \rho^*(\mathfrak{B} (a))_\lambda \beta^* (f)-\beta^*( \rho^*(\mathfrak{B} (a))_\lambda f+\rho^*(a)_\lambda \beta^* (f)+\theta\rho^*(a)_\lambda f) ,v \rangle_\mu\\
    &&\quad=-\langle f,\beta(\rho(\mathfrak{B} (a))_\lambda v)-\rho(\mathfrak{B} (a))_\lambda \beta (v)-(\beta+\theta\mathrm{id})\rho(a)_\lambda\beta (v)\rangle_{\mu-\lambda}.
\end{align*}
Then the first conclusion follows directly from
Proposition~\ref{prop-dual} and the second conclusion follows from the
first conclusion by taking the adjoint representation.
<!-- end proof-8 -->
<!-- proposition-8: body_md -->
\label{pp-rbdr}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ be a
Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra of weight  $\theta$ and $(M,\rho,\mathfrak{T} ) $ be a
representation of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\mathfrak{B})$, where $\mathcal{A}$ and $M$ are
free as   ${\bf k}[\partial]$-modules. Then $(M^{*c},\rho^*,$
$-\theta\mathrm{id}-\mathfrak{T}^*)$  
is a representation of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\mathfrak{B})$. In
particular,  $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},{\rm ad}^*,-\theta \mathrm{id}-\mathfrak{B}^*) $ is a
representation of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\mathfrak{B})$, which is called
the {\bf coadjoint representation} of $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda
\cdot],\mathfrak{B})$.
<!-- end proposition-8 -->
<!-- proof-9: body_md -->
Note that Eq. (\ref{eqtb}) holds when $\beta=-\theta\mathrm{id}-\mathfrak{T}$. Then this conclusion follows directly from Lemma \ref{m8}.
<!-- end proof-9 -->
<!-- definition-12: body_md -->
A {\bf quadratic   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal
algebra of weight  $\theta$} consists of a   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal
algebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot], \mathfrak{B})$  of weight  $\theta$ and a non-degenerate
symmetric invariant conformal bilinear form
$\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$ which satisfies the following
condition
\begin{equation}\label{brblca}
    \omega_\lambda(a,\mathfrak{B}(b))+\omega_\lambda(\mathfrak{B}(a),b)+\theta\omega_\lambda(a,b)=0,\qquad \forall a,b\in \mathcal{A}.
\end{equation}
Denote it by $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B},\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot))$.
<!-- end definition-12 -->
<!-- remark-6: body_md -->
\label{rmk-1}
Let
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B},\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot))$
be a quadratic   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra of weight
$\theta$. By Lemma \ref{bb8}, it is straightforward to show that
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\widehat{\mathfrak{B}},\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot))$
is also a quadratic   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra of weight
$\theta$. Moreover, the existence of a non-degenerate symmetric
invariant conformal bilinear form $\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$
implies that $\mathcal{A}$ is necessarily free as a  ${\bf
k}[\partial]$-module.
<!-- end remark-6 -->
<!-- theorem-2: body_md -->
\label{flrbl}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\Delta_r)$ be a factorizable Lie
conformal bialgebra with $I=r_+-r_-$ and $\theta\in {\bf k}$. Then
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B},\omega^I_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot))$
is a quadratic    Rota-Baxter Lie conformal
algebra of weight  $\theta$, where the ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism
$\mathfrak{B}:\mathcal{A}\rightarrow \mathcal{A}$ and
$\omega^I_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$ are defined respectively by
\begin{align}
    \label{b}       &\mathfrak{B}=\theta r_-\circ I^{-1}, \\
    \label{w}       &\omega^I_\lambda(a,b)=\langle I^{-1}(a),b\rangle_\lambda,  \;\;\;\forall  a,b\in \mathcal{A}.
\end{align}
<!-- end theorem-2 -->
<!-- proof-10: body_md -->
Let $a,b\in \mathcal{A}$. Then we have
\begin{eqnarray}
    I([I^{-1}(a)_\lambda I^{-1}(b)]^r)&=&(r_+-r_-)([I^{-1}(a)_\lambda I^{-1}(b)]^r)\label{eq-q1}\\
    &=&[(I+r_-)(I^{-1}(a))_\lambda (I+r_-)(I^{-1}(b)) ]-[r_-(I^{-1}(a))_\lambda r_-(I^{-1}(b))]\nonumber\\
    &=&[a_\lambda b]+[r_-(I^{-1}(a))_\lambda b]+[a_\lambda r_-(I^{-1}(b))].\nonumber
\end{eqnarray}
Thus we obtain
\begin{align*}
    [\mathfrak{B}(a)_\lambda \mathfrak{B}(b)]&=[\theta (r_-\circ I^{-1})(a)_\lambda \theta (r_-\circ I^{-1})(b) ]
    =\theta^2 (r_-\circ I^{-1}\circ I)([I^{-1}(a)_\lambda  I^{-1} (b)]^r)\\
    &=\theta^2 (r_-\circ I^{-1})(I([I^{-1}(a)_\lambda  I^{-1} (b)]^r))\\
    &=\theta^2 (r_-\circ I^{-1})([a_\lambda b]+[r_-(I^{-1}(a))_\lambda b]+[a_\lambda r_-(I^{-1}(b))])\\
    &=\mathfrak{B}(\theta[a_\lambda b]+[\mathfrak{B}(a)_\lambda b]+[a_\lambda
    \mathfrak{B}(b)]).
\end{align*}
Hence $\mathfrak{B}$ is a Rota-Baxter operator of weight $\theta$
on $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$. Obviously,
$\omega^I_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$ is non-degenerate. By Lemma
\ref{I}, we have
\begin{align*}
    &\omega_\lambda^I(a,b)=\langle I^{-1}(a),b\rangle_\lambda=\langle b,I^{-1}(a)\rangle_{-\lambda}=\langle I^{-1}(b),a\rangle_{-\lambda}
    =\omega_{-\lambda}^I(b,a),\\
    &\omega_{\lambda}^I([a_\mu b],c)=\langle I^{-1}([a_\mu b]),c\rangle_\lambda=-\langle I^{-1}([b_{-\mu-\partial} a]),c\rangle_\lambda =-\langle ad^* (b)_{\lambda-\mu}(I^{-1}(a)),c\rangle_\lambda\\
    &\qquad\qquad\,\,\,\,=\langle I^{-1}(a),[b_{\lambda-\mu}c]\rangle_\mu=\omega_\mu^I(a,[b_{\lambda-\mu
    }c]).
\end{align*}
Hence $\omega^I_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot) $ is a non-degenerate symmetric invariant
conformal bilinear form on $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$. Note that
$r_-^\ast=-r_+$. Therefore we obtain
\begin{eqnarray*}
    &&\omega_{\lambda}^I(a,\mathfrak{B}(b))+\omega_{\lambda}^I(\mathfrak{B}(a),b)+\theta\omega_{\lambda}^I(a,b)\\
    &=&\langle I^{-1} (a),\theta (r_-\circ I^{-1})(b)\rangle_\lambda +\langle I^{-1}(\theta (r_-\circ I^{-1})a,b\rangle_\lambda +\theta\langle I^{-1}a,b\rangle_\lambda\\
    &=&\theta( \langle I^{-1}(a),(r_-\circ I^{-1})(b)+b\rangle_\lambda+\langle (I^{-1}\circ r_-\circ I^{-1})a,b\rangle_\lambda)\\
    &=&\theta\langle a,(I^{-1}\circ r_-\circ I^{-1}+I^{-1}+I^{-1}\circ (-r_+)\circ I^{-1})b\rangle_\lambda=0.
\end{eqnarray*}
So
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B},\omega^I_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot))$
is a quadratic    Rota-Baxter Lie conformal
algebra of weight  $\theta$.
<!-- end proof-10 -->
<!-- corollary-1: body_md -->
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\Delta_r)$ be a factorizable Lie
conformal bialgebra with $I=r_+-r_-$ and $\theta\in {\bf k}$. Then
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\widehat{\mathfrak{B}},\omega^I_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot))$
is a quadratic   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal
algebra of weight  $\theta$, where $\mathfrak{B}$ and $\omega^I_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$
are given by Eqs. \eqref{b} and \eqref{w} respectively.
<!-- end corollary-1 -->
<!-- proof-11: body_md -->
It follows directly from Theorem \ref{flrbl} and Remark \ref{rmk-1}.
<!-- end proof-11 -->
<!-- corollary-2: body_md -->
\label{cfrbl}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\Delta_r)$ be a factorizable Lie
conformal bialgebra with $I=r_+-r_-$ and $\theta\in {\bf
k}\backslash\{0\}$. Let $\mathfrak{B}$ be the Rota-Baxter
operator of weight $\theta$ on $(\mathcal{A}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$
defined by Eq. \eqref{b}. Then
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^\mathfrak{B},\Delta_I)$ is  a Lie
conformal bialgebra, where $$\langle x\otimes y,
\Delta_I(a)\rangle_{\lambda, \mu-\lambda}=\langle [x_\lambda y]^I,
a\rangle_\mu,\;\; \forall a\in \mathcal{A}, x,y\in \mathcal{A}^{*c},$$ and
$$
[x_\lambda y]^I=\theta I^{-1}[(\frac{1}{\theta}I (x))_\lambda  (\frac{1}{\theta}I (y))],\qquad \forall   x,y\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}.
$$
Moreover, $\frac{1}{\theta}I$ is an isomorphism of Lie conformal bialgebras from $(\mathcal{A}^{*c}, \mathcal{A})$ to $(\mathcal{A}_\mathfrak{B}, (\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^I))$.
<!-- end corollary-2 -->
<!-- proof-12: body_md -->
Since $I$ is a Lie conformal algebra isomorphism from $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^r)$ to $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$,  for any $x$, $y\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}$, let $x=I^{-1}(a) $ and $y=I^{-1}(b)$ where $a,b\in \mathcal{A}$. By Eq. (\ref{eq-q1}), we get
\begin{align*}
    &\frac{1}{\theta}I([x_\lambda y]^r)=\frac{1}{\theta}I([I^{-1}(a)_\lambda I^{-1}(b)]^r)\\
    =&\frac{1}{\theta}( [ r_- (I^{-1}(a))_\lambda b]+[a_\lambda r_-(I^{-1}(b))]+[a_\lambda b] )\\
    =&\frac{1}{\theta^2}[a_\lambda b]^\mathfrak{B}=[ \frac{1}{\theta} a_\lambda \frac{1}{\theta} b]^\mathfrak{B}
    =[(\frac{1}{\theta}I(x))_\lambda   (\frac{1}{\theta}I(y))]^\mathfrak{B}.
\end{align*}
Therefore, $\frac{1}{\theta}I$ is a Lie conformal algebra isomorphism from $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^r)$ to $\mathcal{A}_\mathfrak{B}$.  It is obvious that  $ \frac{1}{\theta}I$ is a Lie conformal algebra isomorphism from $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^I)$ to $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$. Note that $ (\frac{1}{\theta}I)^\ast=\frac{1}{\theta}I$. Therefore, $\frac{1}{\theta}I$ is a  Lie conformal bialgebra isomorphism from $(\mathcal{A}^{*c},\mathcal{A})$ to  $(\mathcal{A}_\mathfrak{B},(\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^I)) $.
<!-- end proof-12 -->
<!-- example-2: body_md -->
\label{excdd}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\Delta) $ be a Lie conformal
bialgebra, where $\mathcal{A}$ is free as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module.
Suppose that $(\mathcal{A}^{\ast c}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot]^\ast)$ is the
        Lie conformal algebra in which $[\cdot_\lambda \cdot]^\ast
        $ is defined by Eq.~(\ref{eq:dual}).
Then by Proposition \ref{LCB-equ}, there is a conformal Manin
triple $(\mathcal{A}\oplus \mathcal{A}^{\ast c}, \mathcal{A}, \mathcal{A}^{\ast c})$.  Recall
\cite[Thoerem 3.10]{L} that there is a quasi-triangular Lie
conformal bialgebra on the Lie conformal algebra $\mathcal{D}
=\mathcal{A}\Join\mathcal{A}^{*c}$ with $r=\sum_ie_i\otimes e^*_i\in \mathcal{A}\otimes
\mathcal{A}^{*c}\subset\mathcal{D}\otimes\mathcal{D}$, where $\{e_i\}^n_{i=1}$ is a ${\bf
k}[\partial]$-basis of $\mathcal{A}$ and $\{e^*_i\}^n_{i=1}$ is the dual
${\bf k}[\partial]$-basis of $\mathcal{A}^{*c}$. Then  $r_+$ and $r_-$ are
given by
\begin{equation*}
    r_+(a,x)=x,\qquad r_-(a,x)=-a,\qquad  \forall a\in\mathcal{A},\;\;x\in\mathcal{A}^{*c},
\end{equation*}
which means that $I(a,x)=(r_+-r_-)(a,x)=(a,x)$. Thus
$(\mathcal{D},\mathcal{D}^{*c})$ is a factorizable Lie conformal bialgebra.  Then by
Theorem \ref{flrbl}, there is a quadratic
Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra
$(\mathcal{D},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B},\omega^I_\lambda)$ of weight  $\theta$, where
\begin{align*}
    &\mathfrak{B}(a,x)=(\theta  r_-\circ I^{-1})(a,x)=-\theta a,\\
    &\omega^I_{\lambda}((a,x),(b,y))=\langle I^{-1}(a,x),(b,y ) \rangle_\lambda=x_\lambda(b)+y_{-\lambda}(a),\qquad \forall a,b\in \mathcal{A}, \;\;x,y\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}.
\end{align*}
<!-- end example-2 -->
<!-- theorem-3: body_md -->
\label{rbflca}
Let
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B},\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot))$
be a quadratic   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal
algebra of weight  $\theta$. Let
$I_\omega:\mathcal{A}^{*c}\rightarrow
\mathcal{A}$ be the induced ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module isomorphism given by
$$\langle I^{-1}_\omega a,b
\rangle_\lambda=\omega_\lambda(a,b),\;\; \forall a, b\in \mathcal{A},$$ and
$r\in \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$ with $r_+=\frac{1}{\theta}(\mathfrak{B}+\theta
\mathrm{id})\circ I_\omega:\mathcal{A}^{*c}\rightarrow \mathcal{A}$. Then  $r= \sum_{j=1}^n \frac{1}{\theta}e_j\otimes (\mathfrak{B}+\theta \mathrm{id} )I_\omega(e_j^*)\in \mathcal{A}\otimes \mathcal{A}$  corresponding to $r_+$  is a solution
of the CCYBE in $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$, where $\{e_j\}_{j=1}^n$ is a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-basis of $\mathcal{A}$ and $\{e_j^*\}_{j=1}^n$ is the dual ${\bf k}[\partial]$-basis of $\mathcal{A}^{*c}$.
Thus, there is  a factorizable Lie conformal bialgebra
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta_r)$.
<!-- end theorem-3 -->
<!-- proof-13: body_md -->
Since $\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$ is a symmetric conformal bilinear form,
$(I^{-1}_\omega )^*=I^{-1}_\omega$. By Eq. \eqref{brblca}, we have
$$\mathfrak{B}^*\circ I^{-1}_\omega+I^{-1}_\omega\circ
\mathfrak{B}+\theta I^{-1}_\omega=0. $$ This implies $I_\omega \circ
\mathfrak{B}^*+\mathfrak{B}\circ I_\omega+\theta I_\omega=0$. Thus
$r_+=-\frac{1}{\theta}I_\omega\circ \mathfrak{B}^*$ and
$r_-=-r^*_+=\frac{1}{\theta}\mathfrak{B}\circ I_\omega$. Note that
$r_+-r_-=I_\omega$.

 
                                                                                          
By Remark \ref{rmk-2},  according to
$r_+=\frac{1}{\theta}(\mathfrak{B}+\theta \mathrm{id})\circ I_\omega $, we
set
\begin{align*}
    r&                                                                        
                                                                                                                                                                                                                                            
                                                                                                                          
    =\sum_j \frac{1}{\theta}e_j\otimes (\mathfrak{B}+\theta\mathrm{id})I_\omega (e_j^*).
\end{align*}
 Let $a\in \mathcal{A}$ and $x,y\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}$. Then we have
\begin{eqnarray*}
    &&\langle [x_\lambda y]^r+[y_{-\lambda-\partial}x]^r,a\rangle_\mu\\
    &&\quad=\langle {\rm ad}^*(r_+(x))_\lambda y-{\rm ad}^*(r_-(y))_{-\lambda-\partial}x+ {\rm ad}^*(r_+(y))_{\mu-\lambda} x-{\rm ad}^*(r_-(x))_{\lambda}y ,a\rangle_\mu\\
    &&\quad=-\langle y,[I_\omega (x)_\lambda a]\rangle_{\mu-\lambda}-\langle x,[I_\omega (y)_{\mu-\lambda}a]\rangle_\lambda\\
    &&\quad=\omega_{\mu-\lambda}(I_\omega (y),[a_{-\mu} I_\omega (x)])-\omega_\lambda(I_\omega (x),[I_\omega
    (y)_{\mu-\lambda}a])=0.
\end{eqnarray*}
Hence $$[x_\lambda y]^r=-[y_{-\lambda-\partial}x]^r,\;\; \forall
x, y\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}.$$
For the Jacobi identity, let $x,y,z\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}$ and $a\in \mathcal{A}$. A similar but longer computation shows
that
$$
\langle  [x_\lambda [y_\mu z]^r]^r-[[x_\lambda y]^r_{\lambda+\mu}z]^r-[y_\mu[x_\lambda z]^r]^r ,a\rangle_\nu=0.
$$
 
Therefore, $
(\mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^r)$ is a Lie conformal algebra. By
the invariant property of the conformal bilinear form
$\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$,  $I_\omega\circ {\rm
ad}^*(a)_\lambda={\rm ad}(a)_\lambda\circ I_\omega$. Then by Lemma
\ref{I} (\ref{item2}), the symmetric part of $r$ is
$\mathcal{A}$-invariant.

Furthermore, we have
\begin{align*}
    I_\omega ([x_\mu y]^r)&=I_\omega ({\rm ad}^*( r_+ (x))_\lambda y -{\rm ad}^*(r_-(y))_{-\lambda-\partial}x)
    =[r_+(x)_\lambda I_\omega(y) ]-[r_-(y)_{-\lambda-\partial} I_\omega(x)]\\
    & = [r_+(x)_\lambda ( r_+-r_-)(y)]-[r_-(y)_{-\lambda-\partial}(r_+-r_-)(x)]\\
    & = [r_+(x)_\lambda r_+(y)]-[r_-(x)_\lambda r_-(y)].
\end{align*}
On the other hand, we have
\begin{align*}
    [I_\omega (x)_\lambda I_\omega(y)]^\mathfrak{B}&=[\mathfrak{B}( I_\omega (x))_\lambda I_\omega(y) ]+    [I_\omega (x)_\lambda \mathfrak{B}(I_\omega(y))]+\theta   [I_\omega (x)_\lambda I_\omega(y)]\\
    & =\theta ( [r_-(x)_\lambda  (r_+-r_-)(y)]+[(r_+-r_-)(x)_\lambda r_-(y)]+[(r_+-r_-)(x)_\lambda (r_+-r_-)(y) ])\\
    & =\theta( [r_+(x)_\lambda r_+(y)]-[r_-(x)_\lambda r_-(y)]).
\end{align*}
Hence $\frac{1}{\theta}I_\omega ([x_\lambda
y]^r)=[\frac{1}{\theta}I_\omega (x)_\lambda
\frac{1}{\theta}I_\omega (y)]^\mathfrak{B}$. Since $\mathfrak{B}$
is a Lie conformal algebra homomorphism from $\mathcal{A}_\mathfrak{B}$ to
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$, it follows that $r_-$ is a Lie
conformal algebra homomorphism. Thus by Theorem \ref{qtl}, $
(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta_r)$ is a factorizable Lie
conformal bialgebra where $\Delta_r$ is induced by
$[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^r$.
<!-- end proof-13 -->
<!-- definition-13: body_md -->
Let $(\mathcal{A},\Delta)$ be a Lie conformal coalgebra. A ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism $\mathfrak{B}: \mathcal{A} \rightarrow\mathcal{A}$ is called a {\bf  Rota-Baxter operator  of weight $\theta$ on $(\mathcal{A},\Delta)$ } if it satisfies
    \begin{equation}\label{bd}
        (\mathfrak{B}\otimes \mathfrak{B})\circ\Delta=(\mathfrak{B}\otimes \mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathfrak{B} )\circ\Delta \circ \mathfrak{B} + \theta\Delta \circ \mathfrak{B}.
    \end{equation}
    A Lie conformal coalgebra $(\mathcal{A},\Delta)$ with a Rota-Baxter operator $\mathfrak{B}$ of weight $\theta$ is called a {\bf   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal coalgebra of weight  $\theta$}, denoted by $(\mathcal{A},\Delta,\mathfrak{B})$.
<!-- end definition-13 -->
<!-- remark-7: body_md -->
\label{rmk-rblcca}
    Let $(\mathcal{A},\Delta,\mathfrak{B})$ be a   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal coalgebra of weight  $\theta$,
    where $\mathcal{A}$ is free as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module. It is straightforward to show that $ \mathfrak{B}^*$ is a Rota-Baxter operator of weight $\theta$ on the Lie conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A}^{\ast c},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot]^\ast)$,
     where $[\cdot_\lambda \cdot]^\ast$ is defined by
     Eq.~(\ref{eq:dual}).
<!-- end remark-7 -->
<!-- definition-14: body_md -->
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta)$ be a Lie conformal bialgebra. A ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism $\mathfrak{B}: \mathcal{A} \rightarrow\mathcal{A}$ is called a {\bf  Rota-Baxter operator  of weight $\theta$ on $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta)$ } if $\mathfrak{B}$ is a Rota-Baxter operator of weight $\theta$ on $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ and $\widehat{\mathfrak{B}}$ is a Rota-Baxter operator of weight $\theta$ on $(\mathcal{A},\Delta)$.
<!-- end definition-14 -->
<!-- proposition-9: body_md -->
\label{ppbtb}
    Let     $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ and $(\mathcal{A}',[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]',\mathfrak{B}')$ be two   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal
    algebras of weight  $\theta$. Let  $(\mathcal{A},\mathcal{A}',\rho,\sigma)$ be a matched pair of Lie conformal algebras and  $(\mathcal{A}\Join\mathcal{A}',[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^\Join)$ be the associated Lie conformal algebra where $[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^\Join$ defined by Eq. \eqref{mplb}.
    Then $\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}=\mathfrak{B}\oplus \mathfrak{B}':\mathcal{A}\Join\mathcal{A}'\rightarrow\mathcal{A}\Join\mathcal{A}'$ is a Rota-Baxter operator of weight $\theta$ on the Lie conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A}\Join\mathcal{A}',[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^\Join)$ if and only if  the following conditions hold:
    \begin{align}
        \label{mrb1}&\rho(\mathfrak{B} (a))_\lambda(\mathfrak{B}'( x))=\mathfrak{B}'(\rho (\mathfrak{B} (a ))_\lambda x+\rho(a)_\lambda(\mathfrak{B}' (x))+\theta\rho(a)_\lambda x),\\
        \label{mrb2}&\sigma(\mathfrak{B}'(x))_\lambda(\mathfrak{B} (a))=\mathfrak{B}(\sigma (\mathfrak{B}' (x))_\lambda a+\sigma(x)_\lambda(\mathfrak{B} (a))+\theta\sigma(x)_\lambda a), \qquad \forall a\in\mathcal{A},x\in\mathcal{A}'.
    \end{align}
<!-- end proposition-9 -->
<!-- proof-14: body_md -->
For all $a\in\mathcal{A}$ and $x\in\mathcal{A}'$,  we have
        \begin{align*}
            &[\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}( a,0)_\lambda\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}(0, x) ]^\Join=(-\sigma(\mathfrak{B}'x)_{-\lambda-\partial}(\mathfrak{B} a),\rho(\mathfrak{B} a)_\lambda (\mathfrak{B}' x) ),\\
            &\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}(  [\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}(a,0)_\lambda (0,x)   ]^\Join+[(a,0)_\lambda\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}(0,x)]^\Join+\theta[(a,0)_\lambda(0,x)]^\Join      )\\
            =&(-\mathfrak{B}(\sigma(\mathfrak{B}' x)_{-\lambda-\partial}a+\sigma(x)_{-\lambda-\partial}(\mathfrak{B} a)+\theta\sigma(x)_{-\lambda-\partial}a),\mathfrak{B}'(\rho(\mathfrak{B} a)_\lambda x+\rho(a)_\lambda (\mathfrak{B}' x)+\theta\rho(a)_\lambda x)).
        \end{align*}
        Hence $(\mathcal{A}\Join\mathcal{A}',[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^\Join,\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}})$ is a Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra of weight  $\theta$ if and only if Eqs. \eqref{mrb1} and \eqref{mrb2} hold.
<!-- end proof-14 -->
<!-- theorem-4: body_md -->
\label{th-rblca-flcba}
    Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta)$ be a Lie conformal bialgebra and $\mathfrak{B}$ be a Rota-Baxter operator of weight $\theta$ on  $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta)$  with $\theta\neq 0$, where $\mathcal{A}$ is free as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module.
    Suppose that $(\mathcal{A}^{\ast c}, [\cdot_\lambda \cdot]^\ast)$ is the
        Lie conformal algebra in which $[\cdot_\lambda \cdot]^\ast
        $ is defined by Eq.~(\ref{eq:dual}).
    Let $\{e_i\}_{i=1}^n$ be a ${\bf k}[\partial] $-basis of  $\mathcal{A}$ and $\{e_i^*\}_{i=1}^n$ be the dual ${\bf k}[\partial] $-basis of $\mathcal{A}^{*c}$.
    Then $(\mathcal{A}\Join \mathcal{A}^{*c},\Delta_r)$ is a factorizable Lie conformal bialgebra, where $r$ is defined by
    \begin{equation}\label{eq-r}
        r=-\frac{1}{\theta}\sum_i( \mathfrak{B}(e_i)\otimes e_i^*+e_i^*\otimes \widehat{\mathfrak{B}}(e_i) ).
    \end{equation}
<!-- end theorem-4 -->
<!-- proof-15: body_md -->
By Proposition \ref{LCB-equ}, $(\mathcal{A}\Join \mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^\Join,\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot))$ is a  Lie conformal algebra  with a non-degenerate symmetric invariant conformal bilinear form $\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)$. By Proposition \ref{ppbtb}, $\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}=\mathfrak{B}\oplus \widehat{\mathfrak{B}^*}$ is a Rota-Baxter operator of weight $\theta$ on $(\mathcal{A}\Join \mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^\Join)$.  For all $a,b\in \mathcal{A}$ and $x,y\in \mathcal{A}^{*c}$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\omega_\lambda((a,x),\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}} (b,y))+\omega_\lambda(\widetilde{\mathfrak{B}}(a,x),(b,y))+\theta\omega_\lambda((a,x),(b,y))\\
        =&\langle x,\mathfrak{B}(b)\rangle_\lambda+\langle \widehat{\mathfrak{B}^*}(y),a\rangle_{-\lambda}+\langle \widehat{\mathfrak{B}^*},b\rangle_\lambda+\langle y,\mathfrak{B}(a)\rangle_{-\lambda}+\theta\langle x,b\rangle_\lambda+\theta \langle
        y,a\rangle_{-\lambda}=0.
    \end{align*}
   Hence $(\mathcal{A}\Join \mathcal{A}^{*c},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot]^\Join,\mathfrak{B},\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot))$ is a quadratic Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra of weight  $\theta$.
    Then it follows straightforwardly from Theorem \ref{rbflca}.
<!-- end proof-15 -->
<!-- corollary-3: body_md -->
\label{cor-qrblca-r}
    Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda \cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ be a Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra of weight  $\theta$ with $\theta\neq 0$, where $\mathcal{A}$ is free as a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module. Let $\{e_i\}_{i=1}^n$ be a ${\bf k}[\partial] $-basis of  $\mathcal{A}$ and $\{e_i^*\}_{i=1}^n$ be the dual ${\bf k}[\partial] $-basis of $\mathcal{A}^{*c}$. Then $(\mathcal{A}\ltimes_{{\rm ad}^*} \mathcal{A}^{*c},\Delta_r)$ is a factorizable Lie conformal bialgebra where $r$ is defined by Eq. \eqref{eq-r}.
<!-- end corollary-3 -->
<!-- proof-16: body_md -->
Note that in this case the $\lambda$-bracket on $\mathcal{A}^{\ast c}$ is trivial. Then it follows straightforwardly from Theorem \ref{th-rblca-flcba}.
<!-- end proof-16 -->
<!-- definition-15: body_md -->
\label{plca}\cite{YT}
A {\bf post-Lie conformal algebra} $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],
\cdot_\lambda\cdot)$   is a Lie conformal algebra
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ equipped with a $\lambda$-product
$\cdot_\lambda\cdot: \mathcal{A}\times \mathcal{A} \rightarrow \mathcal{A}[\lambda]$ that
satisfies the conformal sesquilinearity such that the following
conditions hold.
\begin{align}
                                                                                                                                                
    \label{eqpl2}& (a_\lambda b)_{\lambda+\mu}c-(b_\mu a)_{\lambda+\mu}c+[a_\lambda b]_{\lambda+\mu}c+b_\mu(a_\lambda c)- a_\lambda(b_\mu c)=0,\\
    \label{eqpl3}& a_\lambda[b_\mu c]=[(a_\lambda b)_{\lambda+\mu}c]+[b_\mu(a_\lambda c)],  \qquad  \forall   a,b,c\in \mathcal{A}.
\end{align}
<!-- end definition-15 -->
<!-- remark-8: body_md -->
Recall \cite{V} that a {\bf post-Lie algebra} $(A,[\cdot,\cdot],\ast)$ is a Lie algebra $(A,[\cdot,\cdot])$ equipped with a binary operation $\ast:A\times A\rightarrow A$ satisfying
    \begin{align*}
        &a\ast[b,c]=[a\ast b,c]+[b,a\ast c],\\
        &(a\ast b-b\ast a+[a,b] )\ast c=a\ast(b\ast c)-b\ast(a\ast c), \qquad \forall a,b,c\in A.
    \end{align*}
<!-- end remark-8 -->
<!-- definition-16: body_md -->
A conformal bilinear form
$\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)$ on a post-Lie conformal algebra
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\cdot_\lambda\cdot)$ is called {\bf
invariant} if Eq. \eqref{cbf1} and the following condition hold.
\begin{equation}\label{cbf2}
    \omega_{\lambda+\mu} (a_\lambda b,c)-\omega_\lambda(a,b_\mu c)=\omega_{\lambda+\mu}(b_\mu a,c)-\omega_\mu(b,a_\lambda c) ,\qquad\forall a,b,c\in \mathcal{A}.
\end{equation}
A {\bf generalized pseudo-Hessian post-Lie conformal algebra}  $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\cdot_\lambda\cdot,\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot))$   is a post-Lie  conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\cdot_\lambda\cdot)$ equipped with a non-degenerate symmetric invariant conformal bilinear form $\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)$.
<!-- end definition-16 -->
<!-- remark-9: body_md -->
Recall \cite{LBG} that a {\bf generalized pseudo-Hessian post-Lie algebra} $(A,[\cdot,\cdot],\ast, (\cdot,\cdot))$ is a post-Lie algebra $(A,[\cdot,\cdot],\ast)$ equipped with a non-degenerate symmetric bilinear form satisfying
\begin{align*}
    ([a,b],c)=(a,[b,c]),\qquad (a\ast b,c)-(a,b\ast c)=(b\ast a,c) -(b,a\ast c),\qquad \forall a,b,c\in A.
\end{align*}
<!-- end remark-9 -->
<!-- proposition-10: body_md -->
\label{pp-rb-pl}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ be a Rota-Baxter Lie
conformal algebra of weight 1. Define a ${\bf k}$-bilinear map
$\cdot_\lambda\cdot:\mathcal{A}\times \mathcal{A} \rightarrow \mathcal{A}[\lambda]$ by
\begin{equation*}
    a_\lambda b=[\mathfrak{B} (a)_\lambda b],\qquad \forall a,b\in \mathcal{A}.
\end{equation*}
Then $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\cdot_\lambda\cdot)$ is a post-Lie conformal algebra, which is called the {\bf induced post-Lie conformal algebra} from   $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\mathfrak{B})$. Furthermore, if there is a non-degenerate symmetric invariant conformal bilinear form $\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$ on the Lie conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$, then there is a  generalized pseudo-Hessian post-Lie conformal algebra  $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot], \cdot_\lambda\cdot, \omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)) $.
<!-- end proposition-10 -->
<!-- proof-17: body_md -->
The first conclusion follows directly from \cite{YT}.  
In addition, for all $a,b,c\in \mathcal{A}$, we have
\begin{align*}
    \omega_{\lambda+\mu}(a_\lambda b,c)=-\omega_{\lambda+\mu}([b_\mu \mathfrak{B}(a)],c)=-\omega_{\mu}(b,[\mathfrak{B}(a)_\lambda c]) =-\omega_\mu(b,a_\lambda
    c).
\end{align*}
Hence Eq. \eqref{cbf2} holds and thus
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\cdot_\lambda\cdot,
\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)) $ is a generalized pseudo-Hessian
post-Lie conformal algebra.
<!-- end proof-17 -->
<!-- remark-10: body_md -->
A conformal bilinear form $\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)$ on a post-Lie conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\cdot_\lambda\cdot)$ is called {\bf left-invariant} if $\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)$ satisfies Eq. \eqref{cbf1} and
\begin{equation}\label{cbf3}
    \omega_{\lambda+\mu}(a_\lambda b,c)+\omega_\mu(b,a_\lambda c)=0, \qquad \forall a,b,c\in \mathcal{A}.
\end{equation}
 So for the above generalized pseudo-Hessian post-Lie conformal
algebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\cdot_\lambda\cdot,
\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)) $, $\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)$ is
left-invariant on $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\cdot_\lambda\cdot)$.
<!-- end remark-10 -->
<!-- definition-17: body_md -->
\label{df-ppplc}
A {\bf partial-pre-post-Lie conformal algebra (pp-post-Lie
conformal algebra)}
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\lhd_\lambda,\rhd_\lambda)$ is a  Lie
conformal algebra $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ endowed with two
bilinear $\lambda$-products $\lhd_\lambda,\rhd_\lambda: \mathcal{A}\times\mathcal{A}
\rightarrow \mathcal{A}[\lambda]$ satisfying the conformal sesquilinearity
and the following conditions.
\begin{align}
    \label{pppl1}   &a\lhd_\lambda[b_\mu c]=[a_\lambda b]\lhd_{\lambda+\mu}c-[a_\lambda c]\lhd_{-\mu-\partial}b,\\
    \label{pppl2}   &[a_\lambda (b\lhd_\mu c+c\lhd_{-\mu-\partial} b)]=[a_\lambda c]\lhd_{-\mu-\partial}b+b\lhd_\mu [a_\lambda c],\\
    \label{pppl3}   &a\rhd_\lambda[b_\mu c]-[b_\mu c]\lhd_{-\lambda-\partial}a=[(a\rhd_\lambda b+a\lhd_\lambda b)_{\lambda+\mu}c]+[b_\mu (a\rhd_\lambda c-c\lhd_{-\lambda-\partial}a)],\\
    \label{pppl4}   &\{a_\lambda b\}\rhd_{\lambda+\mu}c=a\rhd_\lambda(b\rhd_\mu c)-b\rhd_\mu(a\rhd_\lambda c)+[b_\mu(a\lhd_\lambda c)]-[a_\lambda (b\lhd_\mu c)]-[a_\lambda b]\lhd_{\lambda+\mu}c,\\
    \label{pppl5}   &(a\rhd_\lambda b-b\lhd_\mu a)\lhd_{\lambda+\mu}c+b\lhd_\mu(a\rhd_\lambda c+a\lhd_\lambda c)=a\rhd_\lambda(b\lhd_\mu c)+[a_\lambda(b\lhd_\mu c)],
\end{align}
where $\{a_\lambda b\}= a\rhd_\lambda b +a\lhd_\lambda
b-b\rhd_{-\lambda-\partial} a-b\lhd_{-\lambda-\partial}
a+[a_\lambda b]$, for all $a,b,c\in \mathcal{A}$.
<!-- end definition-17 -->
<!-- remark-11: body_md -->
Note that the notion of partial-pre-post-Lie (pp-post-Lie) algebras was initially introduced in \cite{LBG}, where they were characterized in terms of representations of post-Lie algebras. This illustrates a ``partial"
   splitting of operations of post-Lie algebras, with the conformal generalization presented in Definition \ref{df-ppplc} extending this structure to the conformal context.

Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\lhd_\lambda,\rhd_\lambda)$ be a pp-post-Lie conformal algebra.
\begin{enumerate}
    \item   A pp-post-Lie conformal algebra is called {\bf special},  if $\lhd_\lambda$ is skew-symmetric, i.e., $a\lhd_\lambda b=-b\lhd_{-\lambda-\partial}a $ for all $a,b\in \mathcal{A}$.
    \item If $ \lhd_\lambda$ is trivial, then $(\mathcal{A},\rhd_\lambda,[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ is a post-Lie conformal algebra.
\end{enumerate}
<!-- end remark-11 -->
<!-- proposition-11: body_md -->
\label{phpl-pppl}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\cdot_\lambda\cdot,\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot))$ be a generalized pseudo-Hessian post-Lie conformal algebra. Then there is a  pp-post-Lie conformal algebra structure $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\lhd_\lambda,\rhd_\lambda)$ on $\mathcal{A}$, where $\lhd_\lambda$ and $\rhd_\lambda$ are defined by
\begin{align}
    \label{pppl-cbf1}   &\omega_{\lambda}(a\lhd_\mu b,c)=\omega_{\mu}(a,c_{-\lambda} b),\\
    \label{pppl-cbf2}   &\omega_\lambda(a\rhd_\mu b,c)=-\omega_{\lambda-\mu}(b,a_\mu c-c_{-\mu-\partial}a),     \qquad \qquad\forall a,b,c\in\mathcal{A}.
\end{align}
Moreover, if $\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot) $ is left-invariant, then  $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\lhd_\lambda,\rhd_\lambda)$ is special.
<!-- end proposition-11 -->
<!-- proof-18: body_md -->
Let $a,b,c,d\in \mathcal{A}$. Then we have
\begin{align*}
    &\omega_\nu( a\lhd_\lambda[b_\mu c]-[a_\lambda b]\lhd_{\lambda+\mu}c+[a_\lambda c]\lhd_{-\mu-\partial}b,d  )\\
    =&\omega_\lambda(a ,d_{-\nu}[b_\mu c]  )-\omega_{\lambda+\mu}([a_\lambda b],d_{-\nu}c)+\omega_{\nu-\mu}([a_\lambda c],d_{-\nu}b)\\
    =&\omega_\lambda(a,d_{-\nu}[b_\mu c]-[b_\mu(d_{-\nu}c)]+[c_{\nu-\mu-\lambda}(d_{-\nu}b)])\\
    =&\omega_\lambda(a,d_{-\nu}[b_\mu c]-[b_\mu(d_{-\nu}c)]-[(d_{-\nu}b)_{\mu-\nu}c ]
    )=0.
\end{align*}
Hence Eq. \eqref{pppl1} holds. Similarly, we can verify that Eqs.
\eqref{pppl2} -- \eqref{pppl5} hold and thus the first conclusion
holds. If in addition, $\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)$ is
left-invariant, then we have
\begin{align*}
    \omega_{-\lambda}(c\lhd_{-\lambda-\mu} b,a )=\omega_{-\lambda-\mu}(c,a_\lambda b)= \omega_{\lambda+\mu}(a_\lambda b,c)
    = -\omega_\mu(b,a_\lambda
    c)=-\omega_{-\lambda}(b\lhd_{\mu}c,a).
\end{align*}
Hence $\lhd_\lambda$ is skew-symmetric. Therefore,
$(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\lhd_\lambda,\rhd_\lambda)$ is special.
<!-- end proof-18 -->
<!-- theorem-5: body_md -->
\label{th-ias}
Let $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta_r)$ be a factorizable Lie
conformal bialgebra with $I=r_+-r_-$. Then
 there is a generalized pseudo-Hessian post-Lie conformal algebra
 $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\cdot_\lambda\cdot,\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot))$
 in which $\cdot_\lambda\cdot$ and $\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot)$
 are respectively defined by
    \begin{align*}
        a_\lambda b=[r_-\circ I^{-1}(a)_\lambda b],\qquad \omega_\lambda (a,b)=\langle I^{-1}(a),b\rangle_\lambda, \qquad\forall a,b\in \mathcal{A}.
    \end{align*}
Furthermore, this generalized pseudo-Hessian post-Lie conformal
algebra induces a special pp-post-Lie conformal algebra
    $(\mathcal{A},[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\lhd_\lambda,\rhd_\lambda)$ in which
    $\lhd_\lambda$ and $\rhd_\lambda$ are respectively
    defined by
    $$
    a   \lhd_\lambda b=(r_-\circ I^{-1}+ \mathrm{id})([a_\lambda b]),\qquad a\rhd_\lambda b=[r_-\circ I^{-1}(a)_\lambda b]-a    \lhd_\lambda b,\qquad\forall a,b\in \mathcal{A}.
    $$
<!-- end theorem-5 -->
<!-- proof-19: body_md -->
By Theorem \ref{flrbl}, Proposition \ref{pp-rb-pl} and Proposition \ref{phpl-pppl}, this conclusion holds.
<!-- end proof-19 -->
<!-- proposition-12: body_md -->
\cite[Theorem 2.2]{X} \label{pp-lc-gd}
    Let $A$ be a vector space with binary operations $\cdot$ and $[\cdot,\cdot]$. Then $(R(A)={\bf k}[\partial] A=\bfk[\partial]\ot_\bfk A,[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ is a Lie conformal algebra where the $\lambda$-bracket is defined by
    \begin{equation}\label{eq-lc-gd}
        [a_\lambda b]=\partial(b\cdot a)+\lambda(a\cdot b+b\cdot a)+[a,b],\qquad \forall a,b\in A,
    \end{equation}
    if and only if $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$ is a GDA. $(R(A), [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ is called the {\bf Lie conformal algebra corresponding to (the GDA)}  $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$.
    In particular, if $(A,[\cdot,\cdot])$ is abelian, then $(R(A), [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$  is called the {\bf Lie conformal algebra corresponding to (the Novikov algebra)} $(A,\cdot)$.
<!-- end proposition-12 -->
<!-- definition-18: body_md -->
\cite{HBG}
    A {\bf  Gel'fand-Dorfman bialgebra (GDBA)} is a quintuple $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],$ $\alpha,\beta)$  where $(A,\cdot, \alpha)$ is a Novikov bialgebra and  $(A,[\cdot,\cdot],\beta)$ is a Lie bialgebra and
    the following compatibility conditions hold.
    \begin{align*}
        &(\mathrm{id}\otimes \beta)\alpha(a)-(\tau\otimes \mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id}\otimes \alpha)\beta(a)+(\tau\otimes\mathrm{id})(\mathrm{id}\otimes \beta)\tau\alpha(a)=(\alpha\otimes \mathrm{id})\beta(a)+(\beta\otimes \mathrm{id})\alpha(a),\\
        &\beta(b\cdot a )+\alpha([a,b])=( R(a)\otimes \mathrm{id})\beta(b)+(L(b)\otimes \mathrm{id}+ \mathrm{id}\otimes (L+R)(b))\beta(a)\\
        &\qquad\qquad+({\rm ad}(a)\otimes \mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes {\rm ad}(a))\alpha(b)-(\mathrm{id}\otimes {\rm ad}(b))(\mathrm{id}+\tau)\alpha(a),\qquad  \forall a,b\in A.\nonumber
    \end{align*}
    In addition, if  $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha_r,\beta_r)$ is a GDBA, where $r\in A\otimes A$ and $\alpha_r$ and $\beta_r$ are defined by
    \begin{align}
        \label{nr}&\alpha_r(a)=-(L(a)\otimes \mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes (L+R)(a))r,\\
        \label{lr}& \beta_r(a)=({\rm ad}(a)\otimes \mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes {\rm ad}(a))r, \qquad \forall a\in A,
    \end{align}
    then $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha_r,\beta_r)$ is called {\bf coboundary}.
<!-- end definition-18 -->
<!-- remark-12: body_md -->
Since co-products  $\alpha$ and $\beta$ imply that there is a GDA structure on $A^*$, we always denote the GDBA $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],$ $\alpha,\beta)$ by $(A,A^*)$.
<!-- end remark-12 -->
<!-- proposition-13: body_md -->
\cite[Theorem 2.17]{HBG}\label{lbgb}
    Let $ R(A)={\bf k}[\partial]A$ be the Lie conformal algebra corresponding to a GDA $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$. Suppose that $\alpha$ and $\beta$ are ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module homomorphisms from $ R(A)$ to $ R(A)\otimes  R(A)$ such that $\alpha(A),~\beta(A)\subset A\otimes A$. Then $(  R(A),[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta)$ is a Lie conformal bialgebra with $\Delta$ defined by
    \begin{equation}\label{eq-lcc-gdc}
        \Delta(a)=(\partial\otimes \mathrm{id})\alpha(a)-\tau(\partial\otimes \mathrm{id})\alpha(a)+\beta(a), \qquad \forall a\in A,
    \end{equation}
    if and only if $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha,\beta)$ is a GDBA. We call $(  R(A),[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta)$ the {\bf Lie conformal bialgebra corresponding to the GDBA} $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha,\beta)$.
<!-- end proposition-13 -->
<!-- proposition-14: body_md -->
\label{gdcob}
    Let $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$ be a GDA. Then $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha_r,\beta_r)$ with $r=\sum_i a_i\otimes b_i\in A\otimes A$ is a coboundary GDBA
    if and only if $(A,\cdot,\alpha_r)$ is a coboundary Novikov algebra, $(A,[\cdot,\cdot],\beta_r)$ is a coboundary Lie algebra and the following equations
    hold.
    \begin{eqnarray*}
        &&(L(a)\otimes \mathrm{id}\otimes \mathrm{id} +\mathrm{id}\otimes(L+R)(a)\otimes\mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes\mathrm{id}\otimes(L+R)(a))C(r)-(\mathrm{id}\otimes1\otimes {\rm ad}(a))N(r)\\
        &&\qquad -(\mathrm{id}\otimes {\rm ad}(a)\otimes \mathrm{id})M(r) - \sum_i( (R(a_i)\otimes \mathrm{id})({\rm ad}(a)\otimes \mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes {\rm ad}(a))(r+\tau r)\otimes b_i   \\
        &&\qquad+ a_i\otimes(\mathrm{id}\otimes {\rm ad}(b_i))((L+R)(a)\otimes\mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes L(a)  )(r+\tau r))=0,  \\
        &&(\mathrm{id}\otimes {\rm ad}(b))( (L+R)(a)\otimes\mathrm{id} +\mathrm{id}\otimes L(a))(r+\tau r)=0, \qquad \forall a,b\in A,
    \end{eqnarray*}
where
    $M(r)=N(r)+\tau_{12}N(r)- \sum_i( (L+R)(a_i)\otimes\mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes L(a_i)  )(r+\tau r)\otimes b_i$.
<!-- end proposition-14 -->
<!-- proof-20: body_md -->
It  follows directly from \cite[Theorem 3.3]{HBG}.
<!-- end proof-20 -->
<!-- corollary-4: body_md -->
\label{rmk-m2}
Let $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$ be a GDA and $r\in A\otimes A$. If
$r$ is a solution of the GDYBE  in $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$ and
the symmetric part of $r$  is   $(L,R)$-invariant and {\rm
ad}-invariant, then $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha_r,\beta_r)$ is
a  GDBA, where $\alpha_r$ and $\beta_r$ are defined by Eqs.~{\rm
(\ref{nr})} and {\rm (\ref{lr})} respectively.
<!-- end corollary-4 -->
<!-- proof-21: body_md -->
It follows directly from Proposition \ref{gdcob}.
<!-- end proof-21 -->
<!-- definition-19: body_md -->
Let $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$ be a GDA. If $r\in A\otimes A$ is a
solution of the GDYBE in $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$ and the
symmetric part of  $r$ is both {\rm ad}-invariant and
$(L,R)$-invariant, then we say that the GDBA
$(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha_r,\beta_r)$ with $\alpha_r$ and
$\beta_r$ defined by Eqs. \eqref{nr} and \eqref{lr} respectively
is a {\bf quasi-triangular GDBA}.   In addition,
$(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha_r,\beta_r)$ is called a {\bf
triangular GDBA}, if $r$ is a skew-symmetric solution of the GDYBE
in $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$.
<!-- end definition-19 -->
<!-- proposition-15: body_md -->
Let $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$ be a GDA and the symmetric part of $r=\sum_i a_i\otimes b_i \in A\otimes A$ be {\rm ad}-invariant and $(L,R)$-invariant. Then $r$ is a solution of the GDYBE in $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$ if and only if the maps
    $r^G_+$, $r^G_-:A^{*}\rightarrow A$ are GDA homomorphisms, where
    \begin{equation*}
        r^G_+(x)=\sum_i \langle x,a_i\rangle b_i,\qquad  r^G_-(x)=-\sum_i \langle x,b_i\rangle a_i,\qquad \forall x\in A^*.
    \end{equation*}
In this case,     $(A^{*},\cdot_r,[\cdot,\cdot]_r)$ is a GDA, where $\cdot_r$ and $[\cdot,\cdot]_r$ are defined by
    \begin{equation*}
        [x,y]_r={\rm ad}^*(r^G_+(x))y-{\rm ad}^*(r^G_-(y))x,\qquad x\cdot_r y=(L^*+R^*)(r^G_+(x))y-R^*(r^G_-(y))x,\qquad
        \forall x,y\in A^*.
    \end{equation*}
<!-- end proposition-15 -->
<!-- proof-22: body_md -->
Let $x$, $y\in A^\ast$ and $a\in A$.   Then it is straightforward
to show that $\langle [x,y]_r, a\rangle =\langle x\otimes y,
\beta_r(a)\rangle$ and $\langle x\cdot_r y, a\rangle =\langle
x\otimes y, \alpha_r(a)\rangle$, where $\alpha_r$ and $\beta_r$
are defined by Eqs. (\ref{nr}) and (\ref{lr}) respectively. By
Remark \ref{rmk-m2}, if $r$ is a solution of the GDYBE in
$(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$, then $(A, \cdot, [\cdot,\cdot],
\alpha_r, \beta_r)$ is a GDBA. Therefore,
$(A^{*},\cdot_r,[\cdot,\cdot]_r)$ is a GDA.
    By a straightforward calculation, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\langle z,r^G_+(x\cdot_r y)-r^G_+(x)\cdot r^G_+(y)\rangle=-\langle x\otimes y\otimes z,N(r)\rangle,\\
        &\langle z,r^G_+([x,y]_r)-[r^G_+(x),r^G_+(y)]\rangle=-\langle x\otimes y\otimes
        z,C(r)\rangle.
    \end{align*}
Hence $r^G_+$ is a homomorphism of GDAs from $(A^*, \cdot_r,
[\cdot,\cdot]_r)$ to $(A, \cdot, [\cdot,\cdot])$ if and only if $r$ is a
solution of the GDYBE in $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$.
By   a straightforward calculation,  $r^G_+$ is a GDA homomorphism if and only if
$r^G_-$ is a GDA homomorphism under the condition that the symmetric part of $r$ is  {\rm ad}-invariant and $(L,R)$-invariant.
                  
                                                                 
                                                                  
                                         Thus this conclusion
holds.
<!-- end proof-22 -->
<!-- definition-20: body_md -->
A quasi-triangular GDBA $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha_r,\beta_r)$ is called {\bf factorizable} if the linear map $I_G=r^G_+-r^G_-:(A^*, \cdot_r, [\cdot,\cdot]_r)\rightarrow (A, \cdot, [\cdot,\cdot])$ is an isomorphism of GDAs.
<!-- end definition-20 -->
<!-- theorem-6: body_md -->
\label{th-flgd}
    Let $(R(A)={\bf k}[\partial]A,[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ be the Lie conformal algebra corresponding to a GDA $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$.
    Let $r\in A\otimes A$, which can also be naturally regarded as an element in $ R(A)\otimes  R(A)$.
    Suppose that $\alpha_r$ and $\beta_r$ defined by Eqs. \eqref{nr} and \eqref{lr} respectively are ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module homomorphisms from $ R(A)$ to $R(A)\otimes  R(A)$ such that $ \alpha_r(A),\beta_r(A)\subset A\otimes A$. Then $( R(A),[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta_r)$ is a quasi-triangular Lie conformal bialgebra if and only if $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha_r,\beta_r)$ is a quasi-triangular GDBA. Moreover, $( R(A),[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta_r)$ is a factorizable  Lie conformal bialgebra  if and only if $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha_r,\beta_r)$ is a factorizable GDBA.
<!-- end theorem-6 -->
<!-- proof-23: body_md -->
By \cite[Proposition 3.18]{HBG},  $r\in A\otimes A$  is a solution of the CCYBE in $( R(A),[\cdot_\lambda\cdot])$ if and only if $r$ is a solution of the GDYBE in $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$.
                     
 
    Let $r=\sum_i a_i\otimes b_i$. For all $a\in A$ and $x,y\in A^*$, we have
    \begin{align*}
        &\langle x\otimes y,a_{-\partial^{\otimes^2}} (r+\tau r)\rangle _{\lambda,\mu}\\
        =&\sum_i \langle x\otimes y, [a_{-\lambda-\mu}a_i]\otimes b_i+a_i\otimes [a_{-\lambda-\mu} b_i]+[a_{-\lambda-\mu}b_i]\otimes a_i+b_i\otimes [a_{-\lambda-\mu} a_i]\rangle_{\lambda,\mu}\\
        =&-\langle x\otimes y,(\lambda(L(a)\otimes \mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes (L+R)(a))+\mu( (L+R)(a)\otimes \mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes L(a))) (r+\tau r) \rangle\\
                                                                                           
        &+\langle x\otimes y,({\rm ad}(a)\otimes \mathrm{id}+\mathrm{id}\otimes {\rm ad}(a))(r+\tau
        r)\rangle.
    \end{align*}
   Hence the symmetric part of $r$ is $ R(A)$-invariant if and only if the symmetric part of $r$ is {\rm ad}-invariant and $(L,R)$-invariant. Therefore, $( R(A),[\cdot_\lambda\cdot],\Delta_r)$ is a quasi-triangular Lie conformal bialgebra if and only if $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha_r,\beta_r)$ is a quasi-triangular GDBA.

    Note that $r_+\mid_A =r^G_+$ and $r_-\mid_A=r^G_-$. Therefore, $r^G_+$, $r^G_-:A^*\rightarrow A$ are GDA homomorphisms if and only if $r_+,r_-: R(A)^{*c}\rightarrow  R(A)$ are Lie conformal algebra homomorphisms. Thus $I_G:A^*\rightarrow A$ is a GDA isomorphism if and only if $I : R(A)^{*c}\rightarrow  R(A)$ is a Lie conformal algebra isomorphism. Then this conclusion holds.
<!-- end proof-23 -->
<!-- remark-13: body_md -->
By Theorem \ref{th-flgd}, if $(A, [\cdot,\cdot])$ is trivial, we can get a correspondence between a class of quasi-triangular (resp. factorizable) Lie conformal bialgebras  and quasi-triangular (resp. factorizable) Novikov bialgebras.
<!-- end remark-13 -->
<!-- definition-21: body_md -->
A {\bf    Rota-Baxter GDA}
$(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ of weight  $\theta$ consists of  a  GDA
$(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot])$ and a linear map $\mathfrak{B}: A\rightarrow A$
such that
\begin{equation}\label{lrb}
    \begin{aligned}
        &\mathfrak{B}(a)\cdot\mathfrak{B}(b)=\mathfrak{B}( \mathfrak{B}(a)\cdot b+a\cdot \mathfrak{B}(b)+\theta a\cdot b ),\\
        &[\mathfrak{B}(a),\mathfrak{B}(b)]=\mathfrak{B}( [\mathfrak{B}(a),b]+[a,\mathfrak{B}(b)]+\theta[a,b]),\qquad \forall a,b\in A.
    \end{aligned}
\end{equation}
For convenience, sometimes we also denote it by $(A,\mathfrak{B})$.
<!-- end definition-21 -->
<!-- definition-22: body_md -->
Let $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ be a Rota-Baxter GDA of
weight  $\theta$ and $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],(\cdot,\cdot))$ be a
quadratic GDA. Then $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\mathfrak{B}
,(\cdot,\cdot))$ is called a {\bf quadratic Rota-Baxter GDA of
weight  $\theta$} if  the following compatibility condition holds.
\begin{equation*}
    (\mathfrak{B}(a),b)+    (a, \mathfrak{B}(b))+\theta(a,b)=0,\qquad \forall a,b\in A.
\end{equation*}
In particular, if the Lie bracket in $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\mathfrak{B} ,(\cdot,\cdot))$ is trivial, then $(A,\cdot,\mathfrak{B},(\cdot,\cdot))$ is a {\bf quadratic   Novikov algebra of weight  $\theta$}.
<!-- end definition-22 -->
<!-- proposition-16: body_md -->
\label{pp-qrblca-gda}
Let $(R(A), [\cdot_\lambda \cdot])$ be the Lie conformal algebra
corresponding to a  GDA $(A$, $\cdot$, $[\cdot,\cdot])$, and $\mathfrak{B}$
be a ${\bf k}[\partial]$-module homomorphism from $R(A)$ to $R(A)$
such that $\mathfrak{B}(A)\subset A$. Then $(R(A), [\cdot_\lambda
\cdot],\mathfrak{B})$ is a   Rota-Baxter Lie conformal
algebra  of weight  $\theta$ if and only if $(A, \cdot, [\cdot,\cdot], $ $\mathfrak{B}|_A )$ is a
Rota-Baxter GDA of weight  $\theta$. $(R(A), [\cdot_\lambda \cdot],
\mathfrak{B})$  is called {\bf the   Rota-Baxter Lie
conformal algebra of weight  $\theta$ corresponding to $(A, \cdot, [\cdot,\cdot],
\mathfrak{B}|_A)$}.

In particular, if there is a conformal bilinear form
$\omega_\lambda(\cdot,\cdot)$ defined by
$$
\omega_\lambda(a,b)=(a,b),\qquad \forall a,b\in A,
$$
then $(R(A), [\cdot_\lambda \cdot],$
$\mathfrak{B},\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot))$ is a quadratic
Rota-Baxter Lie conformal algebra of weight  $\theta$ if and only if
$(A, \cdot, [\cdot,\cdot], $ $\mathfrak{B}|_A,(\cdot,\cdot) )$ is a
quadratic Rota-Baxter GDA of weight  $\theta$. $(R(A),
[\cdot_\lambda \cdot], \mathfrak{B},\omega_{\lambda}(\cdot,\cdot))$  is
called {\bf the quadratic   Rota-Baxter Lie
conformal algebra  of weight  $\theta$ corresponding to $(A, \cdot, [\cdot,\cdot],
\mathfrak{B}|_A,(\cdot,\cdot))$}.
<!-- end proposition-16 -->
<!-- proof-24: body_md -->
It is straightforward.
<!-- end proof-24 -->
<!-- theorem-7: body_md -->
\label{th-rbgd-fgd}
Let $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\alpha_r,\beta_r)$ be a factorizable GDBA with $I_G=r^G_+-r^G_-$ and $\theta\in {\bf k}$. Then $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\mathfrak{B},(\cdot,\cdot)^{I_G})$ is a quadratic Rota-Baxter GDA of weight
 $\theta$, where the linear map $\mathfrak{B}:A\rightarrow A$ and the bilinear form $(\cdot,\cdot)^I$ are respectively defined  by
$$
\mathfrak{B}=\theta r_-^G\circ I_{G}^{-1},\qquad (a,b)^{I_G}=\langle I_G^{-1}a, b\rangle, \qquad \forall a,b\in A.
$$
Conversely, Let $(A,\cdot,[\cdot,\cdot],\mathfrak{B},(\cdot,\cdot))$ be a quadratic Rota-Baxter GDA of weight  $\theta$ with $\theta\neq 0\in {\bf k}$.
Let $I_G: A^*\rightarrow A$ be the induced vector space isomorphism given by $(a,b)=\langle I_G^{-1} a,b\rangle $ for all $a,b \in \mathcal{A}$.
